# Cap-selected amplitudes and triangle chords for honeycomb walks

Coordinate `EN-115-000-0020-2-2026-PR-T4-UK-EX-M2-B-PH-M-0486` | `PM-237.13` | status ACTIVE | original slug `Cap-selected-amplitudes-and-triangle-chords-for-honeycomb-walks-September-26-2026` | date 2026-09-26 | derived from the openai/math preprint Cap-selected-amplitudes-and-triangle-chords-for-honeycomb-walks-September-26-2026, modified (Apache-2.0). Publication status: DRAFT.

<!-- PM:BEGIN pm.node.v2 -->
```json
{
  "abstract_verbatim": "At the critical honeycomb fugacity, self-avoiding port-to-port chords in an equilateral lattice triangle of side <i>R</i>, summed over both boundary endpoints and restricted to diameter at least $`R/100`$, have partition sum comparable to <i>R</i><sup>3/4</sup> and mean length $`R^{4/3+o(1)}`$. We also determine the amplitude selected by two vacuum caps on a cylinder of circumference <i>N</i> and prove that it grows as $`N^{1/6+o(1)}`$.",

<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> At the critical honeycomb fugacity, self-avoiding port-to-port chords in an equilateral lattice triangle of side <i>R</i>, summed over both boundary endpoints and restricted to diameter at least $`R/100`$, have partition sum comparable to <i>R</i><sup>3/4</sup> and mean length $`R^{4/3+o(1)}`$. We also determine the amplitude selected by two vacuum caps on a cylinder of circumference <i>N</i> and prove that it grows as $`N^{1/6+o(1)}`$.

- **claim-triangle-chords** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-triangle)): For the critical chord law $\mathbb P_R$, $$Z_R^{\rm chord}\asymp R^{3/4}, \qquad \mathbb E_R m=R^{4/3+o(1)}.$$ The same mean exponent holds for ordinary centre-to-centre self-avoiding walks contained in $\Delta_R$, with both endpoints on its inner boundary layer (the centres adjacent to a boundary port), weighted by $x$ per edge and restricted to have diameter at least $R/100$.
  - Explanation: For the critical chord law in the lattice triangle, the chord partition sum is comparable to a fixed power of the side length, and the mean number of visited centres has a power-law exponent. The same mean exponent holds for ordinary centre-to-centre walks with endpoints on the inner boundary layer.
- **claim-cylinder-amplitude** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-amplitude)): For each sufficiently large $N$, the preceding cylinder sums are finite and $$\mathcal A_N:=\lim_{H\to\infty}\lambda_N^{-H}\mathcal G_N(H) \quad\text{exists in }(0,\infty).$$ Their asymptotics and the planar nesting asymptotic are $$\mathcal A_N=N^{1/6+o(1)}, \qquad \mathcal Z_v(r)=r^{1/12+o(1)}.$$ The bounds are unchanged by a lattice translation of $v$.
  - Explanation: For large circumference, the two-cap cylinder amplitude exists as a limit, is finite and positive, and grows with a power-law exponent. The planar nesting partition sum has a separate power-law exponent, and the bounds are unchanged by lattice translation of the vertex.
- **claim-cylinder-deficit-winding-mass** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-cylinder)): In these port and highest-row conventions, $$D_N:=1-2\cos(3l)\sum_{g=1}^{N-1}K_N(g)\asymp N^{-1/4}, \qquad P_N\asymp N^{-1}.$$ The comparison constants can be chosen independently of the integer circumference $N\ge1$.
  - Explanation: The cylinder boundary deficit and the bare mass of one winding polygon with fixed highest row each have a power-law order in the circumference, with comparison constants independent of the circumference.
- **claim-independent-twist-residue** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-residue)): For generic independent nonzero parameters $K_i$, $$\sum_{D_i=-K_i}\frac{\Phi}{\det(\partial_{t_j}\log D_i)} =(-1)^N\sum_{\mathcal L} \mathrm{Res}_{\mathcal L}\Phi\prod_{i\ {\rm left}}(D_i/K_i).$$ The left sums only in the regular configuration torus (no coincident shift orbits, no coincidences with site orbits); a term on the right assigns all labels injectively to ordered frozen slots: length 0,1,2 at each site, $x-1,x-2$ (called left) or $y+1,y+2$, with second slot occupied only if first occupied. Scalar residues are successive ordinary one-variable residues in $t_i$, earlier slot first.
  - Explanation: For generic independent twists, the sum over regular level solutions of the weight divided by the logarithmic Jacobian equals a signed sum of iterated scalar residues over injective assignments of labels to ordered frozen slots.
- **claim-compactification** (construction, [source](#stmt-cl-compactification)): At each recursive stage, the regular configuration torus admits a smooth compactification, obtained by proper modifications of the projective ratio model, with a simple-normal-crossing boundary containing all coordinate and shifted-collision divisors. Every $D_i^F$ extends to a holomorphic map to $\mathbb P^1$. For generic independent nonzero twists, any collection of level divisors $D_i^F=-K_i$ meets every boundary stratum transversely whenever it meets it at all.
  - Explanation: At each recursive stage the regular configuration torus has a smooth compactification with a simple-normal-crossing boundary containing all coordinate and shifted-collision divisors. Level maps extend to the projective line and meet boundary strata transversely for generic twists.
- **claim-boundary-orders** (bound, [source](#stmt-cl-orders)): The pullback of $\omega_F$ has at worst logarithmic poles along the compactification boundary.
  - Explanation: The pulled-back recursive form has at worst logarithmic poles along the compactification boundary, by an explicit order count over scale cuts, cluster weights and level charges.
- **claim-surviving-slots** (classification, [source](#stmt-cl-surviving-slots)): Group each level pole by its free label. The boundary logarithmic poles can be assigned to those groups so that every intersection selecting one pole from each group consists of disjoint ordinary next-slot collisions and regular level intersections for the remaining labels. No additional collision or coordinate boundary contributes.
  - Explanation: Among boundary logarithmic poles grouped by free label, the only contributing intersections consist of disjoint ordinary next-slot collisions together with regular level intersections; no other collision or coordinate boundary contributes.
- **claim-common-twist-regularity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-common-twist)): Fix sites avoiding one another’s shift orbits. On generic independent-twist fibers define $$\label{eq:common-twist-trace} \tau(f)=\sum_{D_i=-K_i}\frac{Wf}{J}, \qquad J=\det\bigl(\partial_{t_j}\log D_i\bigr).$$ The sum is over the regular configurations of Lemma <a href="#lem:residue" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:residue">4</a>. Then the following assertions hold near a generic diagonal twist $K_1=\cdots=K_N=K\ne0$. In particular, the genericity assumption excludes the toric-end resonances $$\label{eq:common-twist-resonances} (-K)^r=q^{-r^2}\quad\text{or}\quad(-K)^r=q^{r^2}, \qquad 1\le r\le N,\qquad q=e^{2il}.$$* 1. *For every regular Laurent polynomial in the root coordinates, $\tau(f)$ extends holomorphically across the diagonal.* 2. *On the diagonal, the continued trace of any product of site evaluations $L(b)$ equals the trace of the corresponding product of $P(b)$.* 3. *After clearing $Q(w)Q(w-1)(L-P)$ into a polynomial in $z$ by root and spectral monomials as necessary, the specialized trace annihilates every Laurent-polynomial multiple of each of its coefficients.
  - Explanation: Near a generic diagonal twist the trace of regular Laurent polynomials is holomorphic across the diagonal, site evaluations of the transfer function can be replaced by their Laurent polynomial part, and the specialized trace annihilates multiples of the coefficients of the cleared remainder.
- **claim-common-twist-jets** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-common-twist-jets)): Fix the sites and a generic common twist as in Proposition <a href="#prop:common-twist" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:common-twist">8</a>, and denote the specialized trace by $\tau_0$. There are finitely many points $\mathbf p^{(\nu)}\in(\mathbb C^*)^N$, integers $m_\nu\ge0$, and constants $c_{\nu,\alpha}$ such that, for every $f\in\mathcal A$, $$\label{eq:common-twist-jets} \tau_0(f)=\sum_\nu\ \sum_{\|\alpha\|\le m_\nu} c_{\nu,\alpha}\,\partial_{\mathbf p}^{\alpha}f(\mathbf p^{(\nu)}).$$ Here $\alpha\in\mathbb Z_{\ge0}^N$, $\|\alpha\|=\sum_i\alpha_i$, and repeated points are combined and zero local functionals omitted. Every remaining point satisfies the coefficients of the cleared relation $Q(w)Q(w-1)(L-P)=0$; consequently $L=P$ there as a rational identity in the spectral argument. Such a point may have colliding roots. At a regular transverse solution in the original configuration locus, the contribution is the ordinary trace term $Wf/J$, with $J$ as in <a href="#eq:common-twist-trace" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:common-twist-trace">[eq:common-twist-trace]</a>; its weight is nonzero whenever $W\ne0$.
  - Explanation: The specialized trace at a generic common twist is a finite combination of jets of finite order at finitely many points of the root torus, each satisfying the cleared relation; regular transverse solutions contribute the ordinary weight over Jacobian term.
- **claim-all-row-moments** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-row-moments)): Let $N,h\ge0$ be integers. With ordered site variables $\mathbf b=(b_1,\ldots,b_N)$, the right and left vacuum caps $\Psi,\Lambda$, the scalar trace $\tau$, and the Laurent polynomial part $P$ of Sections <a href="#sec:vacuum" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:vacuum">4</a>–<a href="#sec:site-moments" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:site-moments">7</a>, the following is an identity of meromorphic functions of the site variables, nonzero twist $K$, and row variables $u_1,\ldots,u_h$: $$\label{eq:all-row-moments} \Lambda\prod_{a=1}^h T^{K^{-1}}(u_a;\mathbf b)\Psi =\frac{\tau\!\left(\prod_{a=1}^h P(u_a)/d_{\rm full}(u_a)\right)}{\tau(1)}.$$ At a common-twist collision, the trace is the holomorphic specialization near a generic diagonal supplied by Proposition <a href="#prop:common-twist" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:common-twist">8</a>; colliding roots are retained by that continuation. Pointwise evaluation is at regular values of the meromorphic expressions, including repeated row arguments. For $N=0$ the empty site and root conventions apply, and for $h=0$ both sides are one.
  - Explanation: Every product of row transfers between the two vacuum caps, at arbitrary and repeated row arguments, equals the normalized scalar trace of the corresponding product of Laurent polynomial parts divided by their denominators.
- **claim-two-site-fusion-trace** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-scalar-two-site-fusion)): Fix a generic common twist for both lists. For any finite list of row arguments $u_a(\epsilon)$ with limits $u_a(0)$, assume that for every $a$ both limiting denominator evaluations are regular and nonzero: $$d_{{\rm full},0}(u_a(0))\ne0, \qquad d_{{\rm full},{\rm red}}(u_a(0))\ne0.$$ After shrinking the $\epsilon$-disk, the nearby evaluations used below are then regular and nonzero. Put $$f_\epsilon=\prod_a\frac{P_\epsilon(u_a(\epsilon))} {d_{{\rm full},\epsilon}(u_a(\epsilon))}, \qquad f_{\rm red}=\prod_a\frac{P_{\rm red}(u_a(0))} {d_{{\rm full},{\rm red}}(u_a(0))}.$$ Then, at generic values of the remaining parameters, $$\label{eq:scalar-two-site-fusion} \lim_{\epsilon\to0}\frac{\tau_\epsilon(f_\epsilon)}{\tau_\epsilon(1)} =\frac{\tau_{\rm red}(f_{\rm red})}{\tau_{\rm red}(1)}.$$ The identity extends meromorphically in the remaining parameters. Empty products are allowed. The row arguments may vary with $\epsilon$ subject to the stated regularity.
  - Explanation: When two neighbouring sites merge at fusion separation, the normalized scalar trace converges to the normalized trace on the reduced list, because both traces have at most a simple pole with a common nonzero residue factor.
- **claim-merging-site-order** (bound, [source](#stmt-claim-merging-site-order)): For the offset counts of a cluster containing both sites, put $$B=\frac12\sum_{k\bmod8}(m_k-m_{k+1})^2 +\sum_{k\bmod8}m_km_{k+2}.$$ Then $$B-m_{-1}-m_1-m_2-m_4+1\ge0,$$ and equality requires unit support $\{1,2\}$.
  - Explanation: For a cluster containing both merging sites the logarithmic order is nonnegative, and equality forces the unit support on the two offsets next to the collision.
- **claim-real-counting-branch** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-real-counting)): There are absolute constants $N_0,C$ such that, for every $N\ge N_0$ and $\|\delta\|\le1/4$, the consecutive equations $$T(y_i)=i-\tfrac12+\delta,\qquad 1\le i\le N,$$ have exactly one ordered real solution. Its quantiles belong to $\mathcal B_{N,s}$. Throughout $\mathcal B_{N,s}$, the Jacobian $H$ in <a href="#eq:quantile-jacobian" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:quantile-jacobian">[eq:quantile-jacobian]</a> is invertible and $\\|H^{-1}\\|_{\ell^\infty\to\ell^\infty}\le C$. For each fixed $N$, the solution depends locally analytically on $\delta$ and admits local analytic continuation under small analytic perturbations of the equations.
  - Explanation: For large size and twist perturbation in a fixed interval, the consecutive counting equations have exactly one ordered real solution, lying in the quantile rectangle, with an invertible Jacobian having uniformly bounded inverse and local analytic dependence.
- **claim-positive-vacuum-roots** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-positive-vacuum-roots)): For every $N\ge1$ and every multiset $\xi_1,\ldots,\xi_N>0$, there is a unique monic real polynomial $P_0$ of degree $N$ such that $H=BP_0$ has the missing modes. The zeros of $P_0$ are positive and simple.* *Write these zeros as $e^{-2y_j}$, with $y_1<\cdots<y_N$, set $t_j=iy_j$, and put $H_j(z)=H(q^jz)$. For $K\in\mathbb C^*$, the normalized scalar TQ expression for these roots is the rational function $$\label{eq:vacuum-TQ-quotient} F_K(z):=\frac{L(w)}{d_{\rm full}(w)} =\frac{K H_1H_6+H_5H_0+K^{-1}H_4H_7}{H_7H_6}.$$ It satisfies $F_i\equiv1$ and $D_j=-i$ for all $j$. More precisely, with $p,r$ as in Section <a href="#sec:counting" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:counting">9</a>, define $$T_{\boldsymbol\xi}(y) =\sum_{a=1}^N\int_{-\infty}^{y-\eta_a}p(v)\,dv +\sum_{k=1}^N\int_{-\infty}^{y-y_k}r(v)\,dv .$$ The second sum includes the self term. Then $$T_{\boldsymbol\xi}(y_j)=j-\tfrac12+\tfrac14,\qquad 1\le j\le N .$$
  - Explanation: For any positive multiset of site parameters there is a unique monic real polynomial of the stated degree whose product with the site polynomial has the missing modes; its zeros are positive and simple, and they solve the counting equations at the vacuum twist.
- **claim-ordinary-physical-term** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-ordinary-physical-term)): Fix $N\ge N_0$. In a sufficiently small neighborhood of the homogeneous vacuum parameters, take generic sites, a generic common twist $K$ for which the toric ends in Proposition <a href="#prop:common-twist" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:common-twist">8</a> are excluded, and a generic row argument $u$ near $l$. For all sufficiently large $h$, the finite exponential-polynomial expansion of $$G_h=\Lambda\bigl(T^{K^{-1}}(u;\mathbf b)\bigr)^h\Psi$$ contains the term $$\frac{N!a(\mathbf b,K)}{\tau(1)}\,F(u;\mathbf b,K)^h .$$ Its coefficient is nonzero, and no other term has that exponential base. Here $\tau(1)$ is nonzero generically and is independent of $K$.
  - Explanation: For generic parameters near the homogeneous vacuum, the exponential-polynomial expansion of the cap matrix element in the row power contains the ordinary trace term with nonzero coefficient, and no other term has the same exponential base.
- **claim-fixed-n-cap-estimate** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-fixed-n-cap-estimate)): Fix $N\ge N_0$. There is $\varepsilon_N>0$ with the following properties. For every $$p_0=(\mathbf0,u_0,e^{i\theta_0}),\qquad l\le u_0\le l+\varepsilon_N,\quad 0\le\theta_0\le\pi/2,$$ the matrix $\mathsf M_N(p_0)$ has a simple dominant eigenvalue $\lambda(p_0)>0$, and $$\rho\bigl(\mathsf A_N(p_0)\bigr)<\lambda(p_0).$$ The right cap $\Psi$ at twist $i$ and the left cap $\Lambda$ at twist $-i$ are holomorphic in the sites near $\mathbf0$. At homogeneous sites they are limits of rows from vacuum-empty ends at their respective twists, with reversal and spin negation on the upper end as in the definition of $\Lambda$.* *There is a complex neighborhood $\mathcal U$ of $p_0$, positive constants $C,\gamma,\beta$, and holomorphic functions $\lambda,c_{\rm phys}$ on $\mathcal U$, with $$\gamma<\beta<\inf_{p\in\mathcal U}\|\lambda(p)\|,$$ such that, for $p=(\mathbf b,u,K)\in\mathcal U$ and all $h\ge0$, $$\label{eq:fixed-N-cap-bounds} \begin{aligned} \|E^-_h(p)\|+\|E^+_h(p)\|+\|C_h(p)\|&\le C\gamma^h,\\ \|G_h(p)-c_{\rm phys}(p)\lambda(p)^h\|&\le C\beta^h. \end{aligned}$$ Here $\lambda(p)$ continues the stated annular eigenvalue. At $K=i$ in a vacuum neighborhood, $\lambda=1$ and $c_{\rm phys}=1$. The gaps, constants and neighborhoods in this proposition may depend on $N$.
  - Explanation: At fixed size the annular transfer matrix has a simple dominant eigenvalue with a gap below it on the nonempty block; caps are holomorphic in the sites, and end sums and the cap matrix element obey exponential bounds around the leading physical term. The constants may depend on the size.
- **claim-physical-coefficient** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-physical-coefficient)): Fix $N\ge N_0$, homogeneous sites, and $0\le\theta\le\pi/2$. Evaluate $W,J$ at the real roots of Proposition <a href="#prop:real-counting" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:real-counting">13</a> with $\delta=\theta/(2\pi)$, and write the quotient as $(W/J)_\theta$. Put $F_\theta(u)=F(u;\mathbf0,e^{i\theta})$. In the cap matrix element with bulk spin twist $K^{-1}=e^{-i\theta}$, right cap twist $i$, and left cap twist $-i$, the eigenvalue selected in Proposition <a href="#prop:fixed-N-cap-estimate" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:fixed-N-cap-estimate">16</a> is the TQ value $F_\theta(l)$. Its coefficient is $$\label{eq:physical-coefficient} c_{\rm phys}(\theta)=\frac{(W/J)_\theta}{(W/J)_{\pi/2}} .$$ In particular $c_{\rm phys}(\pi/2)=1$.
  - Explanation: The eigenvalue selected by the caps is the TQ value at the physical row, and its coefficient is the ratio of the root-product over Jacobian quotient at the given twist to its value at the vacuum twist, which equals one there.
- **claim-higher-twist-jets** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-v-higher-twist-jets)): For each $N\ge1$, suppose the finite-row eigenvalue has an expansion $$F_N(s,\nu)=1+\sum_{k\ge1}\nu^k j_{N,k}(s), \qquad j_{N,k}(s)=f_{N,k}(h(s)),\qquad \deg f_{N,k}\le N,$$ analytic near $\nu=0$, and satisfies $f_{N,k}(1)=\mathbf1_{k=1}$. Suppose also that every coefficient of $$F_N(s-2 l,\nu)F_N(s+2 l,\nu)-F_N(s,\nu)$$ vanishes to order at least $N$ at $s= l$. Assume the strip-operator facts <a href="#eq:v-jet-strip-representation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:v-jet-strip-representation">[eq:v-jet-strip-representation]</a>–<a href="#eq:v-jet-matrix-inputs" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:v-jet-matrix-inputs">[eq:v-jet-matrix-inputs]</a>. Set, with the index $N$ suppressed, $$\begin{split} H_k(s)&=j_k(s-2 l)+j_k(s+2 l)-j_k(s),\\ P_k(s)&=\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}j_i(s-2 l)j_{k-i}(s+2 l), \qquad G_k=H_k+P_k. \end{split}$$ For each fixed $k\ge1$ there is a constant $C_k$, independent of $N$, such that $$\label{eq:v-jet-full-strip-conclusion} \sup_{s\in S_{5 l}}\|j_k(s)\|\le C_k, \qquad \|G_k(s)\|\le C_k\|B(s)\|^N\quad(s\in S_{3 l}).$$ For every $0<\delta<5 l$ there is also a constant $C_{k,\delta}$, independent of $N$, such that $$\label{eq:v-jet-sharp-conclusion} \|j_k(s)\|\le C_{k,\delta} \min\left\{1,\frac{e^{\alpha_{\mathrm s}\|\Im s\|}}N\right\} \qquad (\|\Re s\|\le5 l-\delta).$$ Finally, $$\label{eq:v-jet-endpoint-conclusion} \|f_{N,k}'(1)\|\le C_k N^{\beta_{\mathrm s}},\qquad \|[\nu^k]\log F_N(- l,\nu)\|\le\frac{C_k}{N}.$$
  - Explanation: For every fixed derivative order, the jets of the finite-row eigenvalue are bounded uniformly in size on the wide strip, decay away from the real axis at the sharp scale, and have endpoint derivative and logarithmic jet bounds of the stated orders.
- **claim-slit-flux** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-slit)): For every interior contact $e$, $$T_e=2Z_e+\sqrt2L_e.$$ Consequently $T_e$ is comparable, with absolute positive constants, both to the unsigned chord mass through $e$ and to the sum of the enclosing polygon partition functions at the two endpoints of the primal tiling side containing $e$.
  - Explanation: For each interior contact, the signed boundary flux through the slit equals a fixed combination of the enclosing polygon partition function and the marked polygon through the contact; consequently it is comparable to the unsigned chord mass through the contact and to the enclosing partition sums at the two ends of the primal side.

Then the following assertions hold near a generic diagonal twist $K_1=\\cdots=K_N=K\\ne0$. In particular, the genericity assumption excludes the toric-end resonances $$\\label{eq:common-twist-resonances}\n (-K)^r=q^{-r^2}\\quad\\text{or}\\quad(-K)^r=q^{r^2},\n \\qquad 1\\le r\\le N,\\qquad q=e^{2il}.$$*\n\n1.  *For every regular Laurent polynomial in the root coordinates, $\\tau(f)$ extends holomorphically across the diagonal.*\n\n2.  *On the diagonal, the continued trace of any product of site evaluations $L(b)$ equals the trace of the corresponding product of $P(b)$.*\n\n3.  *After clearing $Q(w)Q(w-1)(L-P)$ into a polynomial in $z$ by root and spectral monomials as necessary, the specialized trace annihilates every Laurent-polynomial multiple of each of its coefficients.",

          "exact_quote": "Fix sites avoiding one another’s shift orbits. On generic independent-twist fibers define $$\\label{eq:common-twist-trace}\n \\tau(f)=\\sum_{D_i=-K_i}\\frac{Wf}{J},\n \\qquad J=\\det\\bigl(\\partial_{t_j}\\log D_i\\bigr).$$ The sum is over the regular configurations of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:residue\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:residue\">4</a>. Then the following assertions hold near a generic diagonal twist $K_1=\\cdots=K_N=K\\ne0$. In particular, the genericity assumption excludes the toric-end resonances $$\\label{eq:common-twist-resonances}\n (-K)^r=q^{-r^2}\\quad\\text{or}\\quad(-K)^r=q^{r^2},\n \\qquad 1\\le r\\le N,\\qquad q=e^{2il}.$$*\n\n1.  *For every regular Laurent polynomial in the root coordinates, $\\tau(f)$ extends holomorphically across the diagonal.*\n\n2.  *On the diagonal, the continued trace of any product of site evaluations $L(b)$ equals the trace of the corresponding product of $P(b)$.*\n\n3.  *After clearing $Q(w)Q(w-1)(L-P)$ into a polynomial in $z$ by root and spectral monomials as necessary, the specialized trace annihilates every Laurent-polynomial multiple of each of its coefficients.",
          "source_id": "PM-237.13"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Fix sites avoiding one another’s shift orbits. Then the following assertions hold near a generic diagonal twist $K_1=\\cdots=K_N=K\\ne0$. In particular, the genericity assumption excludes the toric-end resonances $$\\label{eq:common-twist-resonances}\n (-K)^r=q^{-r^2}\\quad\\text{or}\\quad(-K)^r=q^{r^2},\n \\qquad 1\\le r\\le N,\\qquad q=e^{2il}.$$*\n\n1.  *For every regular Laurent polynomial in the root coordinates, $\\tau(f)$ extends holomorphically across the diagonal.*\n\n2.  *On the diagonal, the continued trace of any product of site evaluations $L(b)$ equals the trace of the corresponding product of $P(b)$.*\n\n3.  *After clearing $Q(w)Q(w-1)(L-P)$ into a polynomial in $z$ by root and spectral monomials as necessary, the specialized trace annihilates every Laurent-polynomial multiple of each of its coefficients.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cor-common-twist-jets",
        "exact_quote": "Fix the sites and a generic common twist as in Proposition <a href=\"#prop:common-twist\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:common-twist\">8</a>, and denote the specialized trace by $\\tau_0$. There are finitely many points $\\mathbf p^{(\\nu)}\\in(\\mathbb C^*)^N$, integers $m_\\nu\\ge0$, and constants $c_{\\nu,\\alpha}$ such that, for every $f\\in\\mathcal A$, $$\\label{eq:common-twist-jets}\n \\tau_0(f)=\\sum_\\nu\\ \\sum_{|\\alpha|\\le m_\\nu}\n c_{\\nu,\\alpha}\\,\\partial_{\\mathbf p}^{\\alpha}f(\\mathbf p^{(\\nu)}).$$ Here $\\alpha\\in\\mathbb Z_{\\ge0}^N$, $|\\alpha|=\\sum_i\\alpha_i$, and repeated points are combined and zero local functionals omitted. Every remaining point satisfies the coefficients of the cleared relation $Q(w)Q(w-1)(L-P)=0$; consequently $L=P$ there as a rational identity in the spectral argument. Such a point may have colliding roots. At a regular transverse solution in the original configuration locus, the contribution is the ordinary trace term $Wf/J$, with $J$ as in <a href=\"#eq:common-twist-trace\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:common-twist-trace\">[eq:common-twist-trace]</a>; its weight is nonzero whenever $W\\ne0$.",
        "source_id": "PM-237.13"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cor-common-twist-jets",
          "exact_quote": "Fix the sites and a generic common twist as in Proposition <a href=\"#prop:common-twist\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:common-twist\">8</a>, and denote the specialized trace by $\\tau_0$. Every remaining point satisfies the coefficients of the cleared relation $Q(w)Q(w-1)(L-P)=0$; consequently $L=P$ there as a rational identity in the spectral argument. Such a point may have colliding roots. At a regular transverse solution in the original configuration locus, the contribution is the ordinary trace term $Wf/J$, with $J$ as in <a href=\"#eq:common-twist-trace\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:common-twist-trace\">[eq:common-twist-trace]</a>; its weight is nonzero whenever $W\\ne0$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-row-moments",
        "exact_quote": "Let $N,h\\ge0$ be integers. With ordered site variables $\\mathbf b=(b_1,\\ldots,b_N)$, the right and left vacuum caps $\\Psi,\\Lambda$, the scalar trace $\\tau$, and the Laurent polynomial part $P$ of Sections <a href=\"#sec:vacuum\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:vacuum\">4</a>–<a href=\"#sec:site-moments\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:site-moments\">7</a>, the following is an identity of meromorphic functions of the site variables, nonzero twist $K$, and row variables $u_1,\\ldots,u_h$: $$\\label{eq:all-row-moments}\n \\Lambda\\prod_{a=1}^h T^{K^{-1}}(u_a;\\mathbf b)\\Psi\n =\\frac{\\tau\\!\\left(\\prod_{a=1}^h P(u_a)/d_{\\rm full}(u_a)\\right)}{\\tau(1)}.$$ At a common-twist collision, the trace is the holomorphic specialization near a generic diagonal supplied by Proposition <a href=\"#prop:common-twist\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:common-twist\">8</a>; colliding roots are retained by that continuation. Pointwise evaluation is at regular values of the meromorphic expressions, including repeated row arguments. For $N=0$ the empty site and root conventions apply, and for $h=0$ both sides are one.",
        "source_id": "PM-237.13"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-row-moments",
          "exact_quote": "Let $N,h\\ge0$ be integers.       "explanation": "Every product of row transfers between the two vacuum caps, at arbitrary and repeated row arguments, equals the normalized scalar trace of the corresponding product of Laurent polynomial parts divided by their denominators.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-scalar-two-site-fusion",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a generic common twist for both lists. For any finite list of row arguments $u_a(\\epsilon)$ with limits $u_a(0)$, assume that for every $a$ both limiting denominator evaluations are regular and nonzero: $$d_{{\\rm full},0}(u_a(0))\\ne0,\n \\qquad d_{{\\rm full},{\\rm red}}(u_a(0))\\ne0.$$ After shrinking the $\\epsilon$-disk, the nearby evaluations used below are then regular and nonzero. Put $$f_\\epsilon=\\prod_a\\frac{P_\\epsilon(u_a(\\epsilon))}\n                         {d_{{\\rm full},\\epsilon}(u_a(\\epsilon))},\n \\qquad\n f_{\\rm red}=\\prod_a\\frac{P_{\\rm red}(u_a(0))}\n                         {d_{{\\rm full},{\\rm red}}(u_a(0))}.$$ Then, at generic values of the remaining parameters, $$\\label{eq:scalar-two-site-fusion}\n \\lim_{\\epsilon\\to0}\\frac{\\tau_\\epsilon(f_\\epsilon)}{\\tau_\\epsilon(1)}\n =\\frac{\\tau_{\\rm red}(f_{\\rm red})}{\\tau_{\\rm red}(1)}.$$ The identity extends meromorphically in the remaining parameters. Empty products are allowed. The row arguments may vary with $\\epsilon$ subject to the stated regularity.",
        "source_id": "PM-237.13"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-scalar-two-site-fusion",

Put $$f_\\epsilon=\\prod_a\\frac{P_\\epsilon(u_a(\\epsilon))}\n                         {d_{{\\rm full},\\epsilon}(u_a(\\epsilon))},\n \\qquad\n f_{\\rm red}=\\prod_a\\frac{P_{\\rm red}(u_a(0))}\n                         {d_{{\\rm full},{\\rm red}}(u_a(0))}.$$ Then, at generic values of the remaining parameters, $$\\label{eq:scalar-two-site-fusion}\n \\lim_{\\epsilon\\to0}\\frac{\\tau_\\epsilon(f_\\epsilon)}{\\tau_\\epsilon(1)}\n =\\frac{\\tau_{\\rm red}(f_{\\rm red})}{\\tau_{\\rm red}(1)}.$$ The identity extends meromorphically in the remaining parameters.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-real-counting",
        "exact_quote": "There are absolute constants $N_0,C$ such that, for every $N\\ge N_0$ and $|\\delta|\\le1/4$, the consecutive equations $$T(y_i)=i-\\tfrac12+\\delta,\\qquad 1\\le i\\le N,$$ have exactly one ordered real solution. Its quantiles belong to $\\mathcal B_{N,s}$. Throughout $\\mathcal B_{N,s}$, the Jacobian $H$ in <a href=\"#eq:quantile-jacobian\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:quantile-jacobian\">[eq:quantile-jacobian]</a> is invertible and $\\|H^{-1}\\|_{\\ell^\\infty\\to\\ell^\\infty}\\le C$. For each fixed $N$, the solution depends locally analytically on $\\delta$ and admits local analytic continuation under small analytic perturbations of the equations.",
        "source_id": "PM-237.13"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-positive-vacuum-roots",
        "exact_quote": "For every $N\\ge1$ and every multiset $\\xi_1,\\ldots,\\xi_N>0$, there is a unique monic real polynomial $P_0$ of degree $N$ such that $H=BP_0$ has the missing modes. The zeros of $P_0$ are positive and simple.*\n\n\n*Write these zeros as $e^{-2y_j}$, with $y_1<\\cdots<y_N$, set $t_j=iy_j$, and put $H_j(z)=H(q^jz)$. For $K\\in\\mathbb C^*$, the normalized scalar TQ expression for these roots is the rational function $$\\label{eq:vacuum-TQ-quotient}\n F_K(z):=\\frac{L(w)}{d_{\\rm full}(w)}\n =\\frac{K H_1H_6+H_5H_0+K^{-1}H_4H_7}{H_7H_6}.$$ It satisfies $F_i\\equiv1$ and $D_j=-i$ for all $j$. More precisely, with $p,r$ as in Section <a href=\"#sec:counting\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:counting\">9</a>, define $$T_{\\boldsymbol\\xi}(y)\n =\\sum_{a=1}^N\\int_{-\\infty}^{y-\\eta_a}p(v)\\,dv\n   +\\sum_{k=1}^N\\int_{-\\infty}^{y-y_k}r(v)\\,dv .$$ The second sum includes the self term. Then $$T_{\\boldsymbol\\xi}(y_j)=j-\\tfrac12+\\tfrac14,\\qquad 1\\le j\\le N .$$",
        "source_id": "PM-237.13"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-positive-vacuum-roots",
          "exact_quote": "For every $N\\ge1$ and every multiset $\\xi_1,\\ldots,\\xi_N>0$, there is a unique monic real polynomial $P_0$ of degree $N$ such that $H=BP_0$ has the missing modes. For $K\\in\\mathbb C^*$, the normalized scalar TQ expression for these roots is the rational function $$\\label{eq:vacuum-TQ-quotient}\n F_K(z):=\\frac{L(w)}{d_{\\rm full}(w)}\n =\\frac{K H_1H_6+H_5H_0+K^{-1}H_4H_7}{H_7H_6}.$$ It satisfies $F_i\\equiv1$ and $D_j=-i$ for all $j$. More precisely, with $p,r$ as in Section <a href=\"#sec:counting\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:counting\">9</a>, define $$T_{\\boldsymbol\\xi}(y)\n =\\sum_{a=1}^N\\int_{-\\infty}^{y-\\eta_a}p(v)\\,dv\n   +\\sum_{k=1}^N\\int_{-\\infty}^{y-y_k}r(v)\\,dv .$$ The second sum includes the self term.       "exact_statement": "For every $N\\ge1$ and every multiset $\\xi_1,\\ldots,\\xi_N>0$, there is a unique monic real polynomial $P_0$ of degree $N$ such that $H=BP_0$ has the missing modes. The zeros of $P_0$ are positive and simple.*\n\n\n*Write these zeros as $e^{-2y_j}$, with $y_1<\\cdots<y_N$, set $t_j=iy_j$, and put $H_j(z)=H(q^jz)$. For $K\\in\\mathbb C^*$, the normalized scalar TQ expression for these roots is the rational function $$\\label{eq:vacuum-TQ-quotient}\n F_K(z):=\\frac{L(w)}{d_{\\rm full}(w)}\n =\\frac{K H_1H_6+H_5H_0+K^{-1}H_4H_7}{H_7H_6}.$$ It satisfies $F_i\\equiv1$ and $D_j=-i$ for all $j$. More precisely, with $p,r$ as in Section <a href=\"#sec:counting\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:counting\">9</a>, define $$T_{\\boldsymbol\\xi}(y)\n =\\sum_{a=1}^N\\int_{-\\infty}^{y-\\eta_a}p(v)\\,dv\n   +\\sum_{k=1}^N\\int_{-\\infty}^{y-y_k}r(v)\\,dv .$$ The second sum includes the self term. Then $$T_{\\boldsymbol\\xi}(y_j)=j-\\tfrac12+\\tfrac14,\\qquad 1\\le j\\le N .$$",
      "explanation": "For any positive multiset of site parameters there is a unique monic real polynomial of the stated degree whose product with the site polynomial has the missing modes; its zeros are positive and simple, and they solve the counting equations at the vacuum twist.",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **port** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A *port* is the midpoint of a side of a tiling triangle.
- **path length** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Its length $m(\gamma)$ is its number of visited centres.
- **asymptotic equivalence notation** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Here and below $f(R)\asymp g(R)$ means that their ratio lies between two positive constants for all sufficiently large $R$.
- **power-law exponent notation** ([source](#sec:introduction)): The notation $f(R)=R^{a+o(1)}$ means $\log f(R)/\log R\to a$.
- **winding polygon mass P_N** ([source](#sec:cylinder)): Let $P_N$ be the sum of $x^{\|\gamma\|}$ over simple noncontractible polygons in the infinite quotient cylinder whose highest occupied row is a specified row.
- **two-tip cylinder gas** ([source](#pm-h-4)): Let $\mathcal G_N(H)$ be the sum over finite collections of pairwise vertex-disjoint simple honeycomb polygons on this cylinder of two kinds: winding polygons separating the tips, and contractible polygons enclosing exactly one tip.
- **occupied and vacant states** ([source](#pm-h-7)): The state $0$ is vacant and the states $\pm1$ are occupied.

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-local-identities** [claim](#stmt-prop-cylinder) / [proof](#sec:local): Verify rank reductions, inversion and interchange by direct coefficient checks and interpolation, then derive row commutation, exchange, rotation and fusion. -> meromorphic row identities used for fusion and exchange
- **step-cylinder-deficit** [claim](#stmt-prop-cylinder) / [proof](#pm-h-23): Positive half-plane flux, zero-fugacity cap, differentiated fusion, strip operator with matrix bounds, then tail integrals. -> deficit and winding mass orders uniform in circumference
- **step-vacuum-vector** [claim](#stmt-lem-residue) / [proof](#pm-h-26): Scale separation gives an invertible combination of rows on the subspace with empty coefficient zero, which fixes the vector. -> normalized disk vacuum with explicit denominator and fusion recurrences
- **step-compactification** [claim](#stmt-cl-compactification) / [proof](#pm-h-34): Projective ratio model, shifted-collision blowups and a toric subdivision making level maps regular; transversality for generic twists. -> compact model with normal crossing boundary
- **step-boundary-orders** [claim](#stmt-cl-orders) / [proof](#pm-h-38): Additive order calculation along every final ray from scale cuts, pair charges and site charges. -> nonnegative orders with explicit zero cases
- **step-surviving-slots** [claim](#stmt-cl-surviving-slots) / [proof](#pm-h-41): Exclude zero-order rays by sign tests on level charges and use transversality to keep only next-slot singletons. -> classification of contributing boundary intersections
- **step-residue-lemma** [claim](#stmt-lem-residue) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-residue): Grouped global residue theorem, recursion over simultaneous next freezes, then a layering count giving the sign. -> independent-twist residue formula
- **step-common-twist** [claim](#stmt-prop-common-twist) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-common-twist): Exclude toric ends by resonances, show neutral rays have strictly positive order for the marked forms, and use an integrability estimate on proper fibers. -> holomorphic common-twist trace
- **step-common-twist-jets** [claim](#stmt-cor-common-twist-jets) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-common-twist-jets): Pass to branches along a generic line and extract the constant term of Taylor expansions; use comaximal ideals for the cleared relation. -> finite jet representation of the specialized trace
- **step-site-moments** [claim](#stmt-thm-row-moments) / [proof](#sec:site-moments): Resolve spin words by projectors, match to frozen slots and compare with the residue formula for distinct sites. -> cap matrix elements at distinct sites as normalized traces
- **step-two-site-fusion** [claim](#stmt-lem-scalar-two-site-fusion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-scalar-two-site-fusion): Order bound at merging sites, scaled root equations, common residue factor and ideal restriction of insertions. -> normalized trace passes to the reduced list
- **step-row-moments** [claim](#stmt-thm-row-moments) / [proof](#proof-stmt-claim-merging-site-order-2): Add auxiliary pairs, apply the distinct-site identity, remove pairs by two-site fusion on both sides, continue meromorphically. -> all row moments between the caps
- **step-real-counting** [claim](#stmt-prop-real-counting) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-real-counting): Quantile box containment, strict diagonal dominance of the Jacobian in bulk and at edges, then contraction mapping. -> unique real branch with bounded inverse Jacobian
- **step-vacuum-roots** [claim](#stmt-prop-positive-vacuum-roots) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-positive-vacuum-roots): Orthogonality for a positive measure with a variation-diminishing kernel gives positive simple zeros; label phases by scale separation. -> vacuum endpoint roots and counting phases
- **step-ordinary-term** [claim](#stmt-lem-ordinary-physical-term) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-ordinary-physical-term): Use the jet representation, show that the quotient function determines the root polynomial, and isolate the ordinary term. -> isolated ordinary trace term
- **step-cap-estimate** [claim](#stmt-prop-fixed-n-cap-estimate) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-fixed-n-cap-estimate): Perron-Frobenius gap for the annular matrix, end-sum decomposition, resolvent and exceptional-row bounds. -> fixed-size cap estimate with exponential error
- **step-physical-coefficient** [claim](#stmt-prop-physical-coefficient) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-physical-coefficient): Match the isolated term with the cap simple-pole coefficient near the vacuum and continue analytically along the twist. -> coefficient equals the normalized root-product over Jacobian quotient
- **step-asymptotics** [claim](#stmt-thm-amplitude) / [proof](#pm-h-80): Twist derivative of the product via harmonic averaging of bulk limits; determinant contribution via trace series, independent of twist at leading order. -> logarithmic exponent of the cap coefficient
- **step-higher-jets** [claim](#stmt-lem-v-higher-twist-jets) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-v-higher-twist-jets): Induction on the derivative order using the strip operator, a uniform inverse on the weighted sequence space, contour bounds for the forcing and kernel inversion. -> uniform bounds for every fixed twist derivative
- **step-height-moments** [claim](#stmt-thm-amplitude) / [proof](#pm-h-89): Convert derivative bounds into winding pair mass, height bounds, arc depth moments and contractible polygon height moments. -> height and diameter tails for polygons enclosing a tip
- **step-planar-comparison** [claim](#stmt-thm-amplitude) / [proof](#pm-h-94): Identify the cap matrix element with the two-tip gas, bound wall costs, squeeze the cylinder amplitude by planar nesting sums. -> planar nesting exponent
- **step-slit-flux** [claim](#stmt-lem-slit) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-slit): Slit boundary exploration with first-repeat cancellations against gas polygons; real parts of the conservation identity. -> slit flux identity and comparison with polygon partitions
- **step-chord-denominator** [claim](#stmt-thm-triangle) / [proof](#pm-h-101): Strip crossing and triangle flux bounds, half-plane diameter tail, then integration over source ports. -> chord partition sum order
- **step-chord-mean** [claim](#stmt-thm-triangle) / [proof](#pm-h-103): Sum contact masses using planar nesting and the slit comparison, then divide by the denominator; transfer to centre walks by squeeze. -> mean length exponent for chords and centre walks

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-chord-partition-sum**: Under the critical chord law on the equilateral lattice triangle, the chord partition sum is comparable to R to the power 3/4 for all sufficiently large R.
- **inv-chord-mean-length**: Under the same critical chord law, the mean number of visited centres has logarithmic exponent 4/3 as R grows, written R^{4/3+o(1)}.
- **inv-cylinder-amplitude**: The amplitude selected by the two vacuum caps on the cylinder of circumference N exists in (0,infinity) and grows as N^{1/6+o(1)}; the planar nesting sum grows as r^{1/12+o(1)}.
- **inv-cylinder-deficit-winding-mass**: In the stated port and highest-row conventions, the boundary deficit is of order N^{-1/4} and the single winding polygon mass is of order N^{-1}, with comparison constants independent of the circumference.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-fixed-ports-or-typical-length**: Using the mean-length exponent as a statement about a pair of prescribed ports or about the typical length of a chord. -> The law sums both endpoints and every admissible length; the normalization changes when ports are prescribed, and a mean alone gives no matching lower bound on typical length.
- **anti-finite-size-prefactors**: Reading the exponent statements as explicit finite-size formulas with usable prefactors, for instance to size resources or time budgets. -> The notation R^{a+o(1)} only fixes the limit of the logarithmic ratio; the o(1) term and any constant are not quantified.
- **anti-eigenvalue-without-caps**: Inferring the cylinder amplitude from the dominant eigenvalue of the transfer matrix alone, or applying it to other boundary conditions. -> The leading coefficient depends on both boundary states; the dominant eigenvalue does not determine it.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The main results are asymptotic comparisons and exponent statements (partition sum comparable to R^{3/4}, mean R^{4/3+o(1)}, amplitude N^{1/6+o(1)}) obtained by analytic estimates over all large sizes. No finite procedure with an exact output is stated, so there is nothing a bounded computation can decide.

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper proves asymptotic exponents for a critical lattice path law and gives no algorithm, API or data format a library could implement. The nearest practical reading is a scaling reference for simulation code. |
| games_simulation | True | weak | adaptable | inferred | A Monte Carlo simulator of critical self-avoiding chords in a triangle could be checked against the stated partition-sum and mean-length exponents. The paper does not prescribe a sampler, so the use is an inferred validation target, not a construction. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No tool-building construction appears in the paper. The cylinder deficit is an analytic quantity defined for proofs, not a measurable diagnostic with a procedure. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The residue formula is an exact analytic identity used inside proofs. It is not presented as a procedure with a stated cost, so no algorithm is extracted. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Noncrossing matchings index the vacuum space, but only as a spanning set for linear algebra in the proof. The paper proposes no representation, operation set or cost model. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The amplitude exponent is an asymptotic statement about a lattice model. It carries no dataflow, precision or parallelism content a hardware design could use. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Nothing in the paper concerns hardness assumptions, adversaries or secrecy. It studies counting and mean length of lattice paths. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | There is no learning problem, loss or generalisation claim. The results are scaling laws for a statistical-mechanics gas of polygons and paths. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No objective is optimised and no feasibility problem is solved. The diameter restriction only defines which paths enter a partition sum. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper has no processes, messages or consensus. The weight per extra step is a fugacity in a statistical ensemble, not a cost in a networked system. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The closest outside use is as a reference exponent set for statistical-physics benchmarks of the dilute loop model, covered under simulation. No further independent engineering domain is supported by the source. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-large-circumference-only** (hypothesis): The cylinder amplitude and planar nesting asymptotics are asserted only for sufficiently large circumference. The source gives no threshold, so nothing is claimed for small circumferences. Blocked use: ['Applying the amplitude exponent to a specific small circumference', 'Using the result with an explicit cutoff size as if the threshold were known']
- **limit-exponent-only-no-prefactor** (approximation): All size statements are logarithmic-exponent statements. They fix the limit of the logarithmic ratio and give no explicit prefactor, error term or finite-size correction. Blocked use: ['Using the exponents as explicit finite-size formulas to size memory or time budgets', 'Predicting an absolute value of a partition sum or mean length at a given size']
- **limit-mean-without-typical-length** (formal_scope): The length result is a statement about the mean under the law that sums both endpoints and every admissible length. A mean alone gives no matching lower bound on the typical length. Blocked use: ['Reading the mean exponent as the length of a typical chord', 'Applying the mean exponent to prescribed pairs of ports']
- **limit-diameter-restricted-law** (hypothesis): The chord law is restricted to paths of macroscopic diameter, with both boundary endpoints summed and the critical fugacity on the honeycomb lattice in a lattice triangle. Other cutoffs, domains or fugacities are outside the stated regime. Blocked use: ['Applying the exponents to chords without the diameter restriction', 'Applying the exponents off criticality or on a different lattice']
- **limit-heuristic-fixed-length-comparison** (formal_scope): The link between the chord mean exponent and the fixed-length displacement exponent is only a heuristic comparison of scales. It is not a theorem about fixed-length walks. Blocked use: ['Deducing a rigorous fixed-length displacement exponent from the chord mean exponent']
- **limit-coefficient-needs-both-caps** (formal_scope): The leading coefficient is not determined by the dominant eigenvalue; it depends on both boundary states. The amplitude is therefore tied to the two specified vacuum caps and their twists. Blocked use: ['Inferring the cylinder amplitude from the transfer matrix eigenvalue alone', 'Using the amplitude exponent for other boundary conditions on the cylinder']
- **limit-fixed-size-spectral-gap** (formal_scope): The spectral gap and analyticity conclusions for the annular transfer matrix are fixed-size conclusions. They give no uniform gap or uniform analyticity neighbourhood in the circumference. Blocked use: ['Using a gap or analyticity radius uniform in the circumference', 'Interchanging the large-size limit with fixed-size spectral statements']
- **limit-generic-twist-and-toric-resonances** (hypothesis): The common-twist trace regularity and the finite-jet representation are proved only for generic common twists that avoid the toric-end resonances. They are not asserted at the special vacuum or physical twist values, which are reached by analytic continuation. Blocked use: ['Using the finite-jet representation directly at a resonant twist', 'Treating the common-twist trace as holomorphic at twists that violate the genericity assumption']
- **limit-nonexplicit-size-threshold** (unknown): The counting-branch result is stated for sizes beyond an absolute constant whose value is not given. No numerical value of that threshold, or of the accompanying constant, is available. Blocked use: ['Quoting a concrete size above which the unique real branch is guaranteed', 'Treating the bounds on the inverse Jacobian as having an explicit constant']
- **limit-no-uniform-winding-fugacity-radius** (formal_scope): The higher twist derivative bounds are uniform in the circumference for each fixed derivative order, but the analyticity radius in the winding fugacity may depend on the circumference. Only fixed-order coefficients are controlled. Blocked use: ['Summing the twist expansion at a fugacity independent of the circumference', 'Assuming a size-independent radius of convergence in the winding fugacity']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


At the critical honeycomb fugacity, self-avoiding port-to-port chords in an equilateral lattice triangle of side <i>R</i>, summed over both boundary endpoints and restricted to diameter at least $`R/100`$, have partition sum comparable to <i>R</i><sup>3/4</sup> and mean length $`R^{4/3+o(1)}`$. We also determine the amplitude selected by two vacuum caps on a cylinder of circumference <i>N</i> and prove that it grows as $`N^{1/6+o(1)}`$.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

Under the critical fugacity law, the length is not prescribed: each extra step is penalized by its critical fugacity, while the number of possible paths grows. In a bounded domain these effects balance to produce a nontrivial mean length. We study that mean for paths joining the boundary of an equilateral triangle. The two endpoints are summed over, and a diameter restriction selects paths that explore a fixed fraction of the domain.

Let $\mathbb H$ be the honeycomb lattice dual to the tiling by unit equilateral triangles. A *port* is the midpoint of a side of a tiling triangle. A port-to-port path joins successive ports through triangle centres, visits each centre at most once, and has distinct initial and final ports. Its length $m(\gamma)$ is its number of visited centres. We use $$x=(2\cos(\pi/8))^{-1}=(2+\sqrt2)^{-1/2},
 \qquad w(\gamma)=x^{m(\gamma)}.$$ An ordinary centre-to-centre walk visiting $m$ centres has $m-1$ edges, so changing between these two length conventions changes every path weight by the same factor $x$.

For an integer $R\ge1$, let $\Delta_R$ be the closed equilateral triangle of side length $R$ made of tiling triangles. Write $\mathcal C_R$ for the port-to-port paths inside $\Delta_R$ whose initial and final ports belong to its boundary and whose Euclidean diameter is at least $R/100$. We count both endpoint orders; using unoriented paths gives the same probability measure after the factor two cancels. Define $$Z_R^{\rm chord}=\sum_{\gamma\in\mathcal C_R}w(\gamma),
 \qquad
 \mathbb P_R(\gamma)=\frac{w(\gamma)}{Z_R^{\rm chord}}.$$ Here and below $f(R)\asymp g(R)$ means that their ratio lies between two positive constants for all sufficiently large $R$. The notation $f(R)=R^{a+o(1)}$ means $\log f(R)/\log R\to a$.

<a id="stmt-thm-triangle"></a>
<div id="thm:triangle" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1** (Macroscopic triangle chords). *For the critical chord law $\mathbb P_R$, $$Z_R^{\rm chord}\asymp R^{3/4},
 \qquad
 \mathbb E_R m=R^{4/3+o(1)}.$$ The same mean exponent holds for ordinary centre-to-centre self-avoiding walks contained in $\Delta_R$, with both endpoints on its inner boundary layer (the centres adjacent to a boundary port), weighted by $x$ per edge and restricted to have diameter at least $R/100$.*

</div>

This is a statement about the mean under a law that sums both endpoints and every admissible length. Prescribing two ports changes the normalization and requires additional estimates. Likewise, a mean alone does not give a matching lower bound on the typical length. Those distinctions matter because the present proof extracts an integrated contact mass rather than a concentration estimate.

The exponent $4/3$ is the reciprocal of the predicted size exponent $\nu=3/4$ for a uniformly chosen $n$-step honeycomb walk, whose mean-square endpoint displacement is predicted to be $n^{2\nu+o(1)}$ <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[DuminilCopinSmirnov2012](#ref-DuminilCopinSmirnov2012)]. This is a heuristic comparison of scales: the fixed-length displacement law differs from the finite-triangle critical law considered here. A related rigorous estimate of Krachun and Panagiotis bounds the maximal distance from the starting point of a uniform $n$-step honeycomb walk by $O(n/\log n)$ with probability tending to one <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[KrachunPanagiotis2026](#ref-KrachunPanagiotis2026)]; it does not determine the finite-triangle mean.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Critical weights and the amplitude problem

Nienhuis’s dilute $O(n)$ calculation identified the critical point and the associated critical-exponent predictions for planar loop models <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[Nienhuis1982](#ref-Nienhuis1982)]. Duminil-Copin and Smirnov proved that the honeycomb connective constant is $\sqrt{2+\sqrt2}$, using a parafermionic observable whose local cancellation controls boundary path sums <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[DuminilCopinSmirnov2012](#ref-DuminilCopinSmirnov2012)]. We use that local cancellation in a finite exploration form. Including the tangent factor in their observable of spin $5/8$ gives the flux exponent $1-5/8=3/8$ used here. Its phases will be kept explicit, because changing from an exterior source to an interior slit changes the surviving loop weight.

The local transfer matrices belong to the dilute integrable-model setting. Their exchange and fusion identities permit changes of row arguments and of circumference. Grimm and Pearce <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[GrimmPearce1993](#ref-GrimmPearce1993)] give the dilute braid–monoid construction; Zhou and Batchelor <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[ZhouBatchelor1997](#ref-ZhouBatchelor1997)] analyze periodic spectra and the seam weight in the dilute $O(n)$ and Izergin–Korepin setting. Glazman and Manolescu prove boundary two-point invariance for columnwise rhombic half-plane tilings with angles in $[\pi/3,2\pi/3]$ and Yang–Baxter walk weights <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[GlazmanManolescu2017](#ref-GlazmanManolescu2017)]. These results explain the usefulness of deforming local weights while preserving finite identities. In this paper the local matrix identities are proved in the precise gauge used by the boundary states.

The main analytic obstacle is a boundary coefficient. If a finite transfer matrix $T$ has a simple dominant eigenvalue $\lambda$, then a matrix element between two fixed boundary states has leading form $$\Lambda T^H\Psi=c\lambda^H+\text{smaller terms}.$$ Knowing $\lambda$ does not determine $c$: the coefficient depends on both boundary states. Earlier integrable honeycomb adsorption boundaries were studied by Batchelor and Yung <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[BatchelorYung1995](#ref-BatchelorYung1995)]; the caps considered here have their own contraction and normalization. Here $\Psi$ and $\Lambda$ are vacuum states that suppress closed loops outside the marked region. Their twists differ from the twist of the rows between them. The residue formula and the physical selection argument determine this coefficient in the required normalization.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### The positive observables behind the proof

Two polygon sums connect this coefficient to Theorem <a href="#thm:triangle" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:triangle">1</a>. The first is planar. Fix a vertex $v$ of the triangular tiling, equivalently a face centre of $\mathbb H$. Let $\mathcal Z_v(r)$ be the sum over finite collections of pairwise vertex-disjoint simple honeycomb polygons, each enclosing $v$ and lying within Euclidean distance $r$ of $v$. A polygon $P$ has weight $2x^{|P|}$, where $|P|$ is its number of edges, and the empty collection has weight one. Such collections are necessarily nested.

For the cylinder, orient the tiling with horizontal sides and group its triangles into rows of $\pi/3$-rhombi, whose sides are parallel to $0$ and $\pi/3$. The row index increases by one under translation by $e^{i\pi/3}$. For an integer $N\ge1$, identify horizontal translates by $N$. Choose a seam along the row direction between neighboring columns, with tips at the cuts of integer heights $0$ and $H\ge0$.

Let $\mathcal G_N(H)$ be the sum over finite collections of pairwise vertex-disjoint simple honeycomb polygons on this cylinder of two kinds: winding polygons separating the tips, and contractible polygons enclosing exactly one tip. Each polygon $P$ has weight $2x^{|P|}$, and the empty collection has weight one. Define the sum by exhaustion in both row directions.

For comparison, let $L_N(H)$ be the winding-only partition function between the height cuts $0$ and $H$, with no polygon crossing either cut. Its polygons are contained in the slab, have the same weights, and the empty collection is included. Write $$\lambda_N:=\lim_{H\to\infty}L_N(H)^{1/H}.$$ Section <a href="#sec:projection" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:projection">12</a> proves that this limit exists and identifies it with the positive row growth factor. The following result states the cap amplitude and planar nesting estimates in these conventions.

<a id="stmt-thm-amplitude"></a>
<div id="thm:amplitude" class="theorem">

**Theorem 2** (Cylinder amplitude and planar nesting). *For each sufficiently large $N$, the preceding cylinder sums are finite and $$\mathcal A_N:=\lim_{H\to\infty}\lambda_N^{-H}\mathcal G_N(H)
 \quad\text{exists in }(0,\infty).$$ Their asymptotics and the planar nesting asymptotic are $$\mathcal A_N=N^{1/6+o(1)},
 \qquad \mathcal Z_v(r)=r^{1/12+o(1)}.$$ The bounds are unchanged by a lattice translation of $v$.*

</div>

<figure id="fig:seam">

<figcaption>A schematic fundamental rectangle for the cylinder: its vertical sides are identified. The dashed seam has tips at cuts $0$ and $H$; the labels $i$, $1$, and $− i$ give its twist in the three row regions. The surviving contractible polygons enclose one tip, and the surviving winding polygons separate the tips. Dividing by the winding growth retains the two tip contributions. Curves are schematic and do not represent individual lattice edges.</figcaption>
</figure>

\fill (2,1) circle(2pt) node[right] {$0$};
\fill (2,5) circle(2pt) node[right] {$H$};
\node at (.35,.4) {$i$};
\node at (.35,2) {$1$};
\node at (.35,5.65) {$-i$};
\draw[blue!70!black,thick] (2,1) ellipse(.67 and .48);
\draw[blue!70!black,thick] (2,1) ellipse(1 and .7);
\draw[blue!70!black,thick] (2,5) ellipse(.8 and .53);
\draw[orange!80!black,thick] (0,2.8) to[out=20,in=160] (2,3) to[out=-20,in=170] (4,2.8);
\draw[orange!80!black,thick] (0,3.6) to[out=-15,in=170] (2,3.4) to[out=15,in=190] (4,3.6);
\draw[<->] (0,-.4)--(4,-.4) node[midway,below] {circumference $N$};
\node[align=left,anchor=west] at (4.5,5) {contractible polygons\\around one tip};
\node[align=left,anchor=west] at (4.5,3) {winding polygons\\between the tips};
\end{tikzpicture}
```

Visual witness description of PDF page 4 (generated from the PDF page, not source text):

```text
A schematic fundamental rectangle representing a cylinder whose vertical sides are identified. Below the rectangle, a horizontal double-headed arrow indicates "circumference $N$". The rectangle is divided into three row regions labeled along the left border from top to bottom as "$-i$", "$1$", and "$i$". Along the central vertical line, a dashed seam connects two tip points marked as solid black dots: an upper tip labeled "$\bullet H$" and a lower tip labeled "$\bullet 0$". A closed contractible curve encloses $\bullet H$ and another encloses $\bullet 0$, annotated to the right as "contractible polygons around one tip". Two sinuous curves traverse horizontally across the full width between the vertical boundaries between the two tips, annotated to the right as "winding polygons between the tips".
```


The factor two between these exponents has a geometric meaning. When the seam tips are far apart, each tip supports a planar nest, while the winding polygons lie between them. The transfer coefficient records the two tip contributions after the extensive winding growth $\lambda_N^H$ has been removed. Proving this comparison requires height bounds: a cylinder polygon can be too tall, or its planar lift too wide, to belong to a small neighbourhood of one tip. We establish moments of every fixed order before making the projection.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### A complete route through the argument

The proof begins with positive path sums. Section <a href="#sec:local" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:local">2</a> proves the local matrix identities, and Section <a href="#sec:cylinder" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:cylinder">3</a> converts them into two estimates: a cylinder boundary deficit of order $N^{-1/4}$ and winding-polygon mass of order $N^{-1}$ per highest row. The deficit is the eventual source of the chord normalization $R^{3/4}$.

Sections <a href="#sec:vacuum" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:vacuum">4</a>–<a href="#sec:repeated-moments" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:repeated-moments">8</a> determine the coefficient selected by the two caps. We first construct their normalized vectors and their polynomial denominator. A scalar residue formula then expresses products of row transfers between the caps, with an explicit normalization for the empty product. The root twists are initially independent. Resolving collisions proves that the scalar trace is holomorphic near a generic common twist, where colliding roots may contribute derivatives as well as ordinary evaluations. Adding and removing fused pairs of sites then extends the identity from distinct site arguments to arbitrary repeated row arguments by meromorphic continuation. This step is needed to compute powers $T^H$, not just a finite collection of special matrix elements.

Sections <a href="#sec:counting" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:counting">9</a>–<a href="#sec:asymptotics" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:asymptotics">11</a> identify the physical term in that formula. A real counting system has a unique branch in explicit quantile boxes, including the two extreme roots. Polynomial orthogonality identifies the vacuum end of the branch. Positivity of the finite annular transfer and a separate estimate on the two boundary caps select its ordinary residue coefficient at generic parameters. The finite annular gap and cap estimate make the selected coefficient analytic near each point of this real branch, so the identity continues to the homogeneous physical endpoints. The coefficient is a ratio $W/J$ of a root product and a Jacobian determinant, normalized at zero winding fugacity. Its logarithmic asymptotics give the exponent $1/6$.

Section <a href="#sec:projection" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:projection">12</a> obtains arbitrary fixed height moments from higher twist derivatives, projects the cylinder polygons to the plane, and proves Theorem <a href="#thm:amplitude" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:amplitude">2</a>. Section <a href="#sec:chords" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:chords">13</a> completes the positive walk argument. An edge-slit exploration compares the weight of chords visiting an interior contact with the surrounding polygon partition. That contact weight is at most $R^{1/12+o(1)}$ everywhere and has this order throughout a central region. Summing order $R^2$ contacts gives the unnormalized length mass $R^{25/12+o(1)}$. The boundary deficit gives $Z_R^{\rm chord}\asymp R^{3/4}$, and their ratio is the exponent in Theorem <a href="#thm:triangle" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:triangle">1</a>.

The angle $l=\pi/8$ is fixed throughout; integer shifts inside bracketed trigonometric formulas denote multiples of $l$, whereas geometric row heights are integers in the physical lattice.

<a id="sec:local"></a>
### Local transfer matrices and fusion

We first describe the operator for one tile and prove its two rank reductions, its inverse relation, and its interchange identity. The resulting row identities will let us change the number and order of site parameters while keeping control of the transfer matrix.

The local algebra belongs to the integrable dilute-loop tradition. Grimm and Pearce <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[GrimmPearce1993](#ref-GrimmPearce1993)] give a braid–monoid construction of dilute Yang–Baxter operators and explain their gauge equivalence with the Izergin–Korepin vertex model. We specify the gauge and scalar factors used here and verify the identities for the actual cap contractions.

<a id="pm-h-7"></a>
#### The local operator

Set $l=\pi/8,\ x=(2\cos l)^{-1},\ q=e^{2il},\ p=q^{-1}$. Let $V$ have basis $|m\rangle$, $m=0,+1,-1$, with $s|m\rangle=m|m\rangle$. The state $0$ is vacant and the states $\pm1$ are occupied. All transposes below are taken in these bases, without complex conjugation. Put $C=\sum_{m=\pm1}p^m|m,-m\rangle$ and $P|mk\rangle=|km\rangle$, and define the five scalar weights by $$(a,b,h,d,e)(t)=
 \frac{(\sin2l\sin(3l-t),\,\sin2l\sin t,\,\sin(3l-t)\sin t,\,
 \sin(3l-t)\sin(2l-t),\,\sin(t-l)\sin t)}{\sin(2l+t)\sin(3l+t)} .$$ Define $R(t):V^{\otimes2}\to V^{\otimes2}$ by the following rules, where $m,k=\pm1$ and the scalar weights have argument $t$: $$\begin{split}
 |00\rangle&\mapsto |00\rangle+b C,\qquad
 |m0\rangle\mapsto h|m0\rangle+a|0m\rangle,\quad |0m\rangle\mapsto h|0m\rangle+a|m0\rangle,\\
 |mk\rangle&\mapsto d|km\rangle+{\bf1}_{m=-k}p^{-m}(b|00\rangle+eC).
 \end{split}$$ The rules conserve $s_1+s_2$. Write $\check R=R P$.

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
#### Rank reductions and inversion

At the two arguments $2l$ and $3l$, the range of $R$ comes from a smaller space. For $r=2$, let $E=E_2:V\to V^{\otimes 2}$ take $|0\rangle$ to $|00\rangle+xC$ and $|m\rangle$ to $x(|m0\rangle+|0m\rangle)$ for $m=\pm1$. For $r=3$, let $E=E_3:\mathbb C\to V^{\otimes 2}$ take 1 to $|00\rangle+C$. The rank reduction and its compatible action on a third site are $$R(rl)=EE^t P,\quad
 R_{23}(t)R_{13}(t+rl)(E\otimes I)=(E\otimes I)S,\quad S=
 \begin{cases}R(t+l)&r=2,\\I&r=3.\end{cases}$$ Here $S$ acts on the source of $E$ and the third site; for $r=3$ it is the identity on that third site. We also have the inverse relation $R_{12}(t)R_{21}(-t)=I$. All these are meromorphic identities in their arguments.

To verify the rank reductions, substitute $t=rl$ in the five weights and use $C^t C=0$. We give the coefficient checks for the compatible action, since its scalar normalization will be used later. Use subscripts $0,j,*$ for the arguments $t,t+rl,t+l$. For $r=2$, expand both sides on the inputs $|00\rangle,|m0\rangle,|0m\rangle,|mk\rangle$ of $V\otimes V$. The resulting coefficient equalities are grouped in that order in the following table: $$\begin{array}{ll}
 b_0+x a_jd_0=x b_*,& b_j a_0+x h_j h_0=x,\quad b_j h_0+x h_j a_0=x b_*;\\
 h_j+b_j b_0=h_*,&h_j b_0+b_j e_0=0,\quad a_j a_0+h_0=h_*,\\
 x(a_jh_0+a_0)=a_*,&b_j d_0=a_*;\\
 h_j h_0+x b_j a_0=h_*,& h_j a_0+x b_j h_0=x a_*,\quad a_j+x e_j b_0=x a_*,\\
 e_j d_0=h_*,& a_j b_0+x d_j d_0+x e_j e_0=0;\\
 d_j h_0+a_j a_0=d_*,&d_j a_0+a_j h_0=0,\quad h_j d_0=d_*,\\
 x(h_j b_0+b_j)=b_*,& b_j b_0+h_j e_0=e_*,\quad e_j a_0=b_*,\quad e_j h_0=e_* .
\end{array}$$ The four groups occupy $1,2,2,2$ rows. For example, the first group compares the three output types $\sum p^m|0m,-m\rangle,\ \sum p^m|m,-m,0\rangle,\ \sum p^m|m0,-m\rangle$ on input $|00\rangle$; the extra cap terms vanish because $p^2+p^{-2}=0$. Each scalar equality follows on inserting the fractions and using $2\sin A\sin B=\cos(A-B)-\cos(A+B)$ and $8l=\pi$. For $r=3$, the same substitution leaves the first and third groups, with the explicit $x=h_*=1,\ a_*=b_*=0$; these are the required identities when site 3 is vacant and occupied, respectively.

The inverse relation reduces in the same way to $$dd^-=1,\quad aa^-+hh^-=1,\quad ah^-+ha^-=0,\quad b+d b^-=0,\quad
 b b^-+d e^-+e d^-=0,$$ where a superscript minus denotes the argument $-t$. Direct substitution proves all five equalities.

<a id="pm-h-9"></a>
#### Interchange and row identities

We next derive the interchange identity from the two rank reductions. Transpose the fusion relation and then conjugate by the operation reversing all spins. This combined operation leaves $R$ unchanged, while spin reversal takes $E$ to $PE$. It therefore gives the opposite-product intertwining with $E^t P$. Combining the two intertwinings with $R(rl)=EE^tP$ proves $$R_{12}(u-v)R_{13}(u)R_{23}(v)=R_{23}(v)R_{13}(u)R_{12}(u-v)$$ when $u-v=rl$, $r=2,3$. It also holds at $u-v=0$, since $R(0)=P$. Multiplication by the inverse for the $23$ pair identifies this equation with the $132$ relation at differences $u,u-v,-v$. The same three special cases therefore prove it when $u=0,2l,3l$.

These six specializations determine the identity for every $u$. Indeed, after clearing denominators, the powers of $e^{iu}$ in each entry range from $-4$ to $4$ and have one parity: changing $u$ by $\pi$ conjugates by the sign of occupation at site 1. After removing a monomial factor, an entry is therefore a polynomial of degree at most four in $e^{2iu}$. For generic $v$, the six specializations are distinct modulo $\pi$, so interpolation makes every entry zero. Meromorphic continuation gives the asserted identity for all parameters.

For a list of labelled site parameters $\mathbf b=(b_1,\ldots,b_N)$ and $\zeta\in\mathbb C^*$, define the spin row transfer by $$T^\zeta(u;\mathbf b)=\operatorname{Tr}_a\big(\zeta^{s_a} R_{a1}(u-b_1)\cdots R_{aN}(u-b_N)\big).$$ For a fixed twist $\zeta$, these operators commute at all regular row arguments. To see this for $N>0$, conjugate the trace over two auxiliary spaces by their $R$, then use interchange at each site and conservation of the auxiliary spins. The same assertion holds for the empty list with the evident convention. Interchange also gives the adjacent exchange relation $$T^\zeta(u;\ldots,A,B,\ldots)\check R_{j,j+1}(A-B)
 =\check R_{j,j+1}(A-B)T^\zeta(u;\ldots,B,A,\ldots).$$ The analogous cyclic operation is as follows. Rotate the tensor factors one place to the left and multiply by $\zeta^{s_1}$ on the site sent to the end. This map intertwines the row with the cyclically rotated list, by conservation in slot 1 and cyclicity of the auxiliary trace for entries at separate sites. Shifting a site parameter by $\pi$ conjugates the row by the sign of occupation at that site.

Finally, specialize adjacent entries to $d_0+rl,d_0$. The row intertwines through $E_r$ with the shorter list obtained by replacing this pair with $d_0+l$ for $r=2$, or by deleting it for $r=3$. This follows by inverting the fusion identity with site 3 as the auxiliary space and $t=d_0-u$. The row relations, like the local ones, are meromorphic identities in all parameters.

<a id="sec:cylinder"></a>
### Positive boundary mass and winding polygons

We prove two estimates with constants uniform in the circumference: a boundary deficit of order $N^{-1/4}$, and a mass of order $N^{-1}$ for one winding polygon whose highest row is fixed. The first step is to prove that the boundary sums over all depths are finite. We then express the mass of one polygon as a polynomial in a tile weight, constrain that polynomial by fusion, and recover both estimates from a strip integral.

Retain $l=\pi/8$ and $x=(2\cos l)^{-1}$, and put $c=3/8$. Orient the unit triangular tiling with horizontal sides. A boundary port is the midpoint of a triangle side. A port-to-port path visits each triangle at most once and joins two distinct side midpoints through its centre; a path visiting $m$ centres has weight $x^m$. For an integer $g>0$, let $K_\infty(g)$ be the total weight of paths in the lower half-plane from horizontal boundary port $0$ to port $g$. In the quotient by horizontal translation by an integer $N\ge1$, let $K_N(g)$, $1\le g<N$, be the corresponding sum over paths that are self-avoiding in the quotient and whose chosen lift has endpoints $0,g$. In particular, $K_N(g)\le K_\infty(g)$. All domain boundaries and row cuts in this section follow triangle sides.

Group the triangles into rows of $\pi/3$-rhombi, with sides parallel to $0$ and $\pi/3$; the row index increases by one under translation by $e^{i\pi/3}$. Let $P_N$ be the sum of $x^{|\gamma|}$ over simple noncontractible polygons in the infinite quotient cylinder whose highest occupied row is a specified row. Here $|\gamma|$ is the number of visited centres, equivalently the number of polygon edges. Each actual quotient polygon is counted once without orientation. Thus $P_N$ is a bare sum of single-polygon weights; it will be the derivative at winding fugacity zero, with no factor two.

<a id="stmt-prop-cylinder"></a>
<div id="prop:cylinder" class="proposition">

**Proposition 3** (Cylinder deficit and winding mass). *In these port and highest-row conventions, $$D_N:=1-2\cos(3l)\sum_{g=1}^{N-1}K_N(g)\asymp N^{-1/4},
 \qquad
 P_N\asymp N^{-1}.$$ The comparison constants can be chosen independently of the integer circumference $N\ge1$.*

</div>

<a id="pm-h-11"></a>
#### Finite boundary flux and half-plane summability

Consider a convex finite domain tiled by triangles, and fix an entrance port $a$. Give a path of total signed turn $W$ the complex weight $e^{icW}x^m$. The sum of these weights over paths from $a$ to all exit ports is $1$. We recall the local cancellation, including its phases, because the sign changes when the source lies in an interior slit later in the paper.

Introduce one unit of inward flux at $a$, and stop a partial path whenever it reaches a side midpoint, recording whether it points into a triangle or out of one. At a fresh triangle the two extensions turn by $\pm\pi/3$. Their total multiplier is $$2x\cos(c\pi/3)=1.$$ At a previously visited triangle, pair the two stopped paths obtained by reversing the cycle segment. To check their phases, suppose that the earlier arrival-to-exit turn was $+\pi/3$. The closing turn of the cycle is then $-\pi/3$. The prefix from the boundary source arrives from the exterior of that cycle, so the cycle has rotation $-2\pi$: in the counterclockwise alternative the reflex interior sector would contain the arriving prefix. Including the earlier turn gives a net increment $-4\pi/3$, which becomes $+4\pi/3$ on reversal. The pair cancels because $$2\cos(4\pi c/3)=0.$$ The reflected case has the same cancellation. Thus all interior stops cancel and the input flux equals the boundary output.

<a id="eq:cyl-half-plane"></a>
For an exit port $b$ following $a$ counterclockwise, let $\phi_a,\phi_b$ be the lifted counterclockwise boundary tangents. Closing the path along the boundary gives $$W=\phi_b-\phi_a-\pi.$$ Convexity puts $W$ in $[-\pi,\pi]$, so every real output weight is positive. Exhaust the lower half-plane by increasing triangles symmetric about $a$. The two directions along their horizontal base have phases $e^{\pm ic\pi}$, both with real part $\cos(3l)$. Monotone convergence for these base paths, with the other positive exits discarded, gives $$\label{eq:cyl-half-plane}
 2\cos(3l)\sum_{g>0}K_\infty(g)\le1.$$ This finite bound is the input for the annular cap construction. The equality and the endpoint tail estimate will follow after the deficit has been evaluated.

<a id="pm-h-12"></a>
#### Annular diagram rows and the zero-fugacity cap

We first distinguish the two row operators that enter the argument. The spin row $T^\zeta(u;\mathbf b)$ of Section <a href="#sec:local" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:local">2</a> acts on $V^{\otimes N}$, with a twist $\zeta^{s_a}$ in the auxiliary trace. The diagram row defined below acts instead on annular arch patterns. The two descriptions give the same scalars when the open diagram ports are closed with the specified caps and the orientations are summed; we do not identify their state spaces.

A tile has two incoming legs, auxiliary from the right and quantum from below, and the opposite outgoing legs. Its scalar weights are the functions $a,b,h,d,e$ of Section <a href="#sec:local" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:local">2</a>. An empty tile has weight $1$. A single connection from one input to the other stream’s output has weight $a$, a single straight connection has weight $h$, and a cup or cap joining the two inputs or the two outputs has weight $b$. When all four legs are occupied, the double pairing of $a$-type has weight $d$, and the other double pairing has weight $e$. Contractible closed loops have weight zero, and simple winding loops have weight $n$. At the physical argument $t=l$, $$(a,b,h,d,e)(l)=(x,x^2,x^2,x^2,0).$$ These are exactly the weights of dual self-avoiding paths in the two triangles of a split rhombus.

Here is the orientation comparison with the spin matrix. Choose $\zeta\ne0$ with $n=\zeta+\zeta^{-1}$ and orient each occupied arc. The spins $+1,-1$ mean respectively along and against the positive stream direction. Straighten the positive stream tangents at the tile ports to parallel forward directions, with the auxiliary stream to the right of the quantum stream at entry and to its left at exit. A through connection then has net turn zero. A clockwise cup or cap has phase $p=e^{-i\pi/4}$, and a counterclockwise one has phase $p^{-1}$, exactly as in the terms involving $C$ and $p^{-m}$ in $R(t)$. These spin phases use one quarter of the turn angle, separately from the boundary flux phase $e^{icW}$ above. The changes of tangent used in straightening cancel at each join, so a closed curve of signed rotation $\Theta$ has phase $e^{i\Theta/4}$. Equivalently, the phase counts signed cups and caps and is unchanged by isotopy fixing the port tangents.
